ParlaMint-AT_2008-04-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3033	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-04-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3049	Madam President. Dear High House! Values Advisors! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) The Government Convention provides for the introduction of an Austrian-wide uniform system for the protection of young people. However, according to the provisions of the Austrian Federal Constitution on Youth Protection, it is reserved for the countries both in terms of legislation and enforcement, and therefore we began to invite the countries in the event of excessive alcohol consumption by young people at the beginning of 2007 in order to achieve a uniform regime here with all country representatives. There have been several rounds of meetings on this subject. Text proposals have been prepared on the part of my department. However, the representatives of the countries expressly opposed a uniform system in the Youth Protection Laws. The agreement could be reached on specific points of care and prevention. Of course, I have set up various platforms to continue to maintain cooperation here, and I also advocate the harmonisation of the law on the protection of young people. From my point of view, however, this long-term demand for uniform, simple and, above all, understandable youth protection provisions can best be realized only by means of a constitutional change, since, in my experience of the last one and a half years, a non-constitutional harmonization will hardly be possible because of the different views of each country.

What further biometric data is provided for and to what extent it is a signature, a signature, in which it is, please, beautiful – and I probably misinterpreted it; for this I also apologise when it has arrived – is not about the fingerprint as a fingerprint, which is then brought together in an Austrian-wide file, experts will clarify. It's about the recognition of the e-card. There are working groups in the main association, together with my department, and we will, of course, try to provide the most cost-effective but also the most secure measure for patients, so that we can also guarantee that abuse is pushed back as far as possible; a 100% exclusion will never be possible.

I don't think that's what I'm saying. Sometimes I even have the impression that it even makes people who aren't so far away from me. This is, of course, a networked structure, and it is nothing against it if, in a reasonably regulated market economy, large structures develop for certain tasks of producing or implementing, for some medium-sized ones, for some small ones. This is not the problem at all, nor is it so much about whether in Austria we have too much large, too much small, too much medium-sized enterprises, but it is about what the Austrian economy is supposed to produce and do – and: Can it do that with this structure? My conviction is that on this occasion we need to clear the entire policy areas so to speak. And you will certainly not be surprised if, in this context, I take out two examples – I now confine myself to two – namely energy policy and tax policy. Firstly, on the field of energy policy and on the question, which Mr Bartenstein has always stressed, and the e-control, which is more in his sphere of influence than it is economically reasonable for a control authority to say: Under the Ecostrom Act, the electricity is getting a little more expensive, as if there were no such law. This may perhaps be true for the post-calculation period, for example in recent years, which may be true for the next or the next year, but one can already argue whether these calculation methods really show the additional costs in the form or whether it is not the case that, if special calculation methods were used, the top-load feed would be used and the like would come to different numbers. But that leads too far, I understand. The point is, however, that in the medium term it is quite clear what will happen: The raw materials, which cannot grow up, i.e. fossil energy sources, can only become even more expensive in the medium and long term, with any economically reasonable view. We already have to buy the raw material ourselves, while renewable energy sources are just about the application, the conversion of energy and the technology available for it. This is, of course, at the beginning, in the introduction phase, more expensive, but gradually and in the long run cheaper, which is quite logical. That means: We have to do with fossil raw materials with a development where it can only become more expensive – and in the case of renewables, it is so that they can only be cheaper. So it's just a question of time until the curves cut. This intersection is there faster than you have always predicted. You have also claimed a few years ago that all the experts say – I think you said that in 2003 or 2004 – that the oil price will anyway stabilize at $40! – Nothing with your forecast! And that doesn't surprise you either. The question now is: If that is the case, then we risk or take into account the positive and productive fact that perhaps one year or another – but not more long – from the customers, the households, the producing companies have a slight additional cost, but on a longer, even medium-term basis, a change is more favourable, and also for the customers – let alone for those companies that are engaged in this segment. There, ladies and gentlemen, Austria has a huge potential. We have several companies – I just need to talk about my federal state – that do not only research with high-tech equipment in this respect, but already offer all over the world, interestingly but hardly in Austria, because there is hardly a demand market here; far too little anyway. These are opportunities for businesses and jobs! Now I would like to say a few words about the tax measures, because after-saids will certainly address them. We all know that the so-called wage-related taxes in Austria – in any case internationally – are too high; we are at the forefront if you always want to perform benchmarking. There are various ways to get down there, and one is precisely the ecological and social tax reform we are proposing. Another aspect is of course – and I would also like to address this – to move more in the case of a tax reform than always to use only one volume. Perhaps 3 billion euros of tax reform you, I think, are projecting, or at the end of the day, only 2 billion is not so much if you always try to reduce something there or there, without counter-financing. This produces much less volume. For 10 or 15 years now, the Chamber of Commerce and also the ÖVP business spokesmen – mostly men – have told us that we have to do something about it. And now we are experiencing from tax reform to tax reform that nothing goes further, because there are always some clients being used, but not in the structure of the economy with the probably most important lever of economic policy, namely tax policy. They are simply too little to use or fit in with your conservative ideology to put all assets-related taxes at zero as possible. However, this is against another basic value that you might hold high there or there, namely against the principle of efficiency. This also concerns the medium-sized economy. If we could reduce the wage-related taxes with the same money that we do not lose or even take in addition to them, then the medium-sized economy would also have some of it, which employs a lot of people in Austria; Thank God, you can only say. That would be just as cost-effective. But then we would not only have a justice aspect inside, which can be viewed individually, but also an efficiency or an efficiency aspect. Allocation aspect, as the economic operators say, because then less distortion is done, because then more costs can be taken down where investment is to be made. And these are still the opportunities for job creation – after usual teaching. That's the point where it's relocated. And this is especially true, especially in the Austrian structure, of the medium and small enterprises, if they still have employees, because otherwise they cannot be relieved. What is happening to one-person companies, we will then make the application already announced. Thank you.

All in all: We need a good, innovative disability equalisation law – not at some time, but at the latest at the latest in 2009 with the date of entry into force, because I am generous, 1. [[Intervocations at the ÖVP.]] The purpose of the Audit Guidelines is to make the auditor’s obligations clearer and clearer, to strengthen the requirements for his independence and his professional ethics and to introduce an obligation for external quality assurance and public supervision of the profession of auditors. This is to be welcomed in principle. The following points are mentioned here: responsibility of the consolidated auditor for included individual accounts; the independence of the auditor; Consideration of the auditor's network; and the Audit Directive also extends the rules for independence to the members of the Auditor's Network. In the interests of transparency and independence, these points should at least be welcomed. This also applies to a temporary ban on the activity of the examiner. There is also a regulation in § 270 UGB as regards the auditor's fee. The Group welcomes the implementation of Directive 2006/46 as regards the annual accounts of companies of certain legal forms. This will also strengthen confidence in the annual and consolidated accounts. The following points are of crucial importance here: increasing the threshold value, disclosure of transactions with related persons and out-of-balance sheet transactions, an internal control and risk management system and collective responsibility of organ members. In general, these are quite reasonable changes in the UGB, and for this reason the Liberal Group will also give its approval to these amendments. Like you said, you accused us of polemics yesterday. In this sense, I can now also very objectively reject this polemic. If there are reasonable things – and they sometimes exist on the part of the European Union – then they can also be implemented. But there is no need for an EU reform treaty, but only a reasonable transnational cooperation. If it is reasonable in the matter, can anyone be convinced, Mr Jarolim, or not? In this sense, we shall agree here. Thank you.

It is also intended to ensure that the Management Board bears responsibility for the information in the annual financial statements and for the progress report, so that overall confidence in the now better audited annual and consolidated accounts will be increased. Furthermore, the professional ethics of the auditors and the public supervision of the profession of the auditor should be increased. The present amendments therefore concern company, company and cooperative revision law. Political and, in particular, economic policy is important for this law to strengthen confidence in the annual and consolidated accounts of economically significant companies and thus strengthen Austria's economic location. If Austria's economic location is strengthened, it will ultimately have a positive impact on our employees, on the labour market and on Austria as an overall economic location. In particular, the bill of law brings significant improvements to the cooperative law, which, as regards the regulation on the Supervisory Board, is approach to the Stocks Act and the GmbH Act. In future, a similar regulation of the internal organisation of the Supervisory Board will also be provided for in the cooperative law. Dear Ladies and gentlemen, important provisions also concern the auditor's fee, since in future this audit fee must not be influenced by the auditor's additional performance and must not be linked to any conditions. It should also be standardised that the fee must be proportionate to the duties of the examiner and the expected extent of the test. It should no longer be feared that, in the case of undue low audit fees, the independence and impartiality of the examiner could be at risk. That is important, ladies and gentlemen. It also implements a point from the government programme, and that is a good thing. So far, these voluntary judicial auctions of properties and building rights have been virtually unused. It is currently the case that they are carried out by the notary or notary on behalf of the Court of First Instance, and there are no more than 10 cases per year on average. The notaries, notaries, lawyers and lawyers have therefore for some time been pressing for a change of law because it promises new fields of responsibility. These demands are now to be met. Among other things, lawyers and lawyers are entitled to auction real estate. What is it about in detail? – Voluntary participation is to be extended from the judicial procedure and transferred to the exclusive competence of the notaries and notaries. In addition, the seller or the seller should be able to order certain traders authorised to carry out the actual auction process. For example, lawyers or lawyers or auction houses could be operating. In these cases, the notary or the notary shall then monitor compliance with the statutory requirements. Apart from that, the auctioning procedure should remain essentially unchanged. The auction is based on the compulsory auction of properties, unless the auction provides otherwise. The provisions of the Rules of Procedure shall, of course, be complied with, and the execution and auction shall be carried out by the notary or by the notary. With him or her, for example, he or she has the highest bidder to lie down. The present draft was discussed in detail, including the interests of lawyers and notaries. In the judicial committee, all parties agreed, and we were able to reach a unanimous decision. ==References====External links== January 2009. Voluntary counterfeiting is to become more attractive and thus an alternative to the traditional sale of properties. ParlaMint-AT_2008-04-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e17674	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The objective of this European Convention, which we are ratifying today, is essentially to strengthen children's rights. The most important point is that the Convention contains a number of procedural measures to strengthen children's and children's rights when it comes to supporting them in their right to hearing or in their right to express opinions in court. The great advantage is that in Austria, with the Childhood Law Amendment Act, we have already created, as it were, the legal basis for mediation in 2001 and 2003, namely, these rights to hearing and expressing opinions. That is why, I believe, we are on our way, because there are still a number of countries that have not yet ratified it. So we're on our way. I would like to discuss another point that is directly related to this, because we have also debated this in the Committee. As in the field of We have discussed the still open novels and the necessary reforms to the Maintenance Advancement Act, where we are on the right track and for which the Minister has already done good preparatory work, but for which we lack the money from the Minister of Finance. We must work on this, I believe, but there is an urgent need for innovation. For example, if you look at the numbers of the platform for single-parents and these say that only every second mother receives maintenance regularly for her child or her children, you can see the need for it and that there are still many open questions and gaps. For example, 17 percent receive neither maintenance nor a maintenance advance, and this results in the risk of poverty being particularly high for single parents, which is why a third of the 250 000 single parents in Austria are at risk of poverty. The problem - the Minister has clearly defined this in the Committee - lies, for example, in the Duration of the proceedings until a corresponding maintenance advance is paid, for example, because it may take weeks, months, sometimes even years, until the hopeless execution is determined. – I just recently had another case where the father who was supposed to pay for the maintenance, wherever the title had been established, has changed his place of residence and the district in which he lies regularly, whereby the finding of the hopeless execution begins over and over again and the woman has not received the money she actually owes for months. There is the problem that it takes a very long time for the father's performance to be established, because there are always situations where men who actually have to pay say: That doesn't correspond to my performance! – This makes the whole thing more difficult for the children, because they suffer from the fact that the mother is at risk of poverty and she herself. Furthermore, there is the problem that the maintenance advance ends when the children are 18 years old, regardless of whether they are still in education or not. So there are a number of problems that arise where I think it is time – and we know it and we are all convinced – that we need to solve them relatively quickly in order to prevent child poverty and to stop women’s poverty. We'll need the money for it, but I think we'll have a decision-making template before the summer. - Thank you.

This decision should, in particular, ensure greater civil and customer-friendliness, but also greater legal certainty. I understand the dissatisfaction of the Ministry of Justice with the fact that in the case of exploding health costs, the 50:50-cost target between the federal government and countries has no longer succeeded. This is, of course, something different from the countries and, therefore, the municipalities, according to experience. At the beginning of this agreement, countries still have between 60 and 65 percent of the costs, which is currently up to 30 percent due to the increase in costs. This shows, among other things, how dramatic the cost development in the health sector is. That is why the question here is also whether the current system, in which the criminal service has to carry all medical services during detention, has long since been in need of reform. If you see that from the point of view of the execution of the sentence, it would be better if the prisoners were to be socially insured. However, consideration is already being given to this, and a working group has already been initiated in this regard. I would like to end by thinking on this subject: If a prisoner is to be transferred to a hospital, there must not only be his medical care and therefore be financed, but also his cost-intensive surveillance. The question therefore arises whether an own prison hospital for Austria does not appear more cost-effective under the rule. The existing hospitals in the Stein Penal Detention Centre and in the national prison house in Vienna-Josefstadt are very close to it. I therefore urge a commercial calculation in this respect, in the interests of the best possible discharge of the State budget.
